MEDIUM		Who is subject to the dietary supplement CGMP requirements of 21 CFR Part 111?
key & evidence

G 21 CFR 111.1
H —
J Manufacture, package, label, or hold a dietary supplement; includes those who hold for distribution; excludes certain retailers/holders as specified.

  • SUPPORTED · Manufacture, package, label, or hold a dietary supplement · 111.1(a)
    you are subject to this part if you manufacture, package, label, or hold a dietary supplement, including:
  • PARTIALLY_SUPPORTED · includes those who hold for distribution · 111.1(a)(1)
    A dietary supplement you manufacture but that is packaged or labeled by another person; and
    Holding is covered generally by 111.1(a), but the enumerated inclusions are (1) supplements packaged/labeled by another person and (2) imported supplements; the text does not speak of 'hold for distribution'. J omits the import inclusion in (a)(2). Text not truncated.
  • SUPPORTED · excludes certain retailers/holders as specified · 111.1(b)
    do not apply to you if you are holding those dietary supplements at a retail establishment for the sole purpose of direct retail sale to individual consumers
    Exclusion is limited to the holding requirements only, and a retailer's warehouse is not a retail establishment.

Research needed

  1. Decide whether J's 'hold for distribution' element is an acceptable paraphrase of 111.1(a) or should be flagged; J omits 111.1(a)(2) imports.

source: batch reviewer, first pass

Citation plainly governs, but J's 'includes those who hold for distribution' does not match the enumerated inclusions in 111.1(a)(1)-(2), and J omits the import inclusion.materially uncertain (NEEDS_RESEARCH)

    J omits electronic records and the part 11 requirement in 111.605(c).

G 21 CFR 117.135
H —

  • SUPPORTED · parameters and values · 117.135(c)(1)(ii)
    The maximum or minimum value, or combination of values, to which any biological, chemical, or physical parameter must be controlled to significantly minimize or prevent a hazard
    Parameters and values are required of process controls only, which is part of the distinction the question asks about.
  • SUPPORTED · application to identified hazards · 117.135(a)(1)
    You must identify and implement preventive controls to provide assurances that any hazards requiring a preventive control will be significantly minimized or prevented

G 21 CFR 117.150
H —
J Corrective action procedures, identify and correct, evaluate affected food, prevent recurrence, distinguish corrections from corrective actions, records.

  • SUPPORTED · Corrective action procedures · 117.150(a)(1)
    You must establish and implement written corrective action procedures that must be taken if preventive controls are not properly implemented
  • SUPPORTED · identify and correct · 117.150(a)(2)(i)
    Appropriate action is taken to identify and correct a problem that has occurred with implementation of a preventive control;
  • SUPPORTED · evaluate affected food · 117.150(a)(2)(iii)
    All affected food is evaluated for safety; and
  • SUPPORTED · prevent recurrence · 117.150(a)(2)(ii)
    Appropriate action is taken, when necessary, to reduce the likelihood that the problem will recur;
  • SUPPORTED · distinguish corrections from corrective actions · 117.150(c)
    Corrections. You do not need to comply with the requirements of paragraphs (a) and (b) of this section if:
    117.150(c)(1) limits the corrections route to allergen and sanitation controls, and (c)(2) to minor, isolated problems. A metal detector failure would typically fall outside (c)(1), which matters for the scenario.
  • SUPPORTED · records · 117.150(d)
    All corrective actions (and, when appropriate, corrections) taken in accordance with this section must be documented in records.

  • SUPPORTED · environmental monitoring · 117.165(a)(3) (supplemental)

    Applies if contamination of a ready-to-eat food with an environmental pathogen is a hazard requiring a preventive control. Not cited in G.
  • SUPPORTED · reanalysis · 117.155(a)(5)
    Reanalysis in accordance with § 117.170.
  • SUPPORTED · records review · 117.165(a)(4) (supplemental)

    Not cited in G.

H —

  • PARTIALLY_SUPPORTED · selection based on hazard severity and supplier performance · 117.410(d)(1); 117.425 (supplemental)

    Factors: hazard analysis including 'the nature of the hazard', the entity applying controls, supplier performance, and other factors such as storage and transport. The text does not say 'severity' and the list is broader than J. Supplemental 117.425 only routes back to 117.410(d).

Column J issue: J's selection element names 'hazard severity and supplier performance'; 117.410(d)(1) requires considering the hazard analysis (including the nature of the hazard), the entity applying controls, supplier performance, and other factors. 'Severity' is not the regulation's word and the factor list is narrower. Not a contradiction.

E-001
Direct Lookup		ACCEPTED
MEDIUM		What is the scope of 21 CFR Part 11 and to which records does it apply?
key & evidence

G 21 CFR 11.1; 11.2
H —
J Records in electronic form created, modified, maintained, archived, retrieved, or transmitted under predicate rules; electronic signatures; permitted use.

  • SUPPORTED · Records in electronic form created, modified, maintained, archived, retrieved, or transmitted under predicate rules · 11.1(b)
    This part applies to records in electronic form that are created, modified, maintained, archived, retrieved, or transmitted, under any records requirements set forth in agency regulations.
    The text says 'any records requirements set forth in agency regulations'. 'Predicate rules' is interpretive shorthand for that phrase. (b) also covers records submitted under the FD&C Act and PHS Act and excludes paper records sent electronically.
  • SUPPORTED · electronic signatures · 11.1(a)
    The regulations in this part set forth the criteria under which the agency considers electronic records, electronic signatures, and handwritten signatures executed to electronic records
  • SUPPORTED · permitted use · 11.1(d)
    Electronic records that meet the requirements of this part may be used in lieu of paper records, in accordance with § 11.2, unless paper records are specifically required.
    11.2(a)-(b) set the conditions.

source: batch reviewer, first pass

11.1 and 11.2 are the scope and implementation sections, and every J element is stated in them.

HIGH		What definitions distinguish an open system from a closed system under Part 11?
key & evidence

G 21 CFR 11.3(b)
H —
J Control of system access by persons responsible for the record content.

  • SUPPORTED · Control of system access by persons responsible for the record content (closed system) · 11.3(b)(4)
    Closed system means an environment in which system access is controlled by persons who are responsible for the content of electronic records that are on the system.
  • SUPPORTED · Control of system access by persons responsible for the record content (open system) · 11.3(b)(9)
    Open system means an environment in which system access is not controlled by persons who are responsible for the content of electronic records that are on the system.

G 21 CFR 1.500; 1.501; 1.502

    Importer means the U.S. owner or consignee of an article of food that is being offered for import into the United States.
    The time-of-entry element is in the 'U.S. owner or consignee' definition in 1.500.

    the requirements in this subpart apply to all food imported or offered for import into the United States and to the importers of such food
  • SUPPORTED · exemptions · 1.501(c)
    This subpart does not apply to food that is imported for research or evaluation use

    the recall strategy will specify the level in the distribution chain to which the recall is to extend
  • SUPPORTED · public warning · 7.42(b)(2)

  • SUPPORTED · effectiveness checks · 7.42(b)(3)
    The purpose of effectiveness checks is to verify that all consignees at the recall depth specified by the strategy have received notification about the recall
  • SUPPORTED · firm-initiated recall notification · 7.46(a)
    A firm that does so because it believes the product to be violative is requested to notify immediately the appropriate Food and Drug Administration district office
    Notification is 'requested', not mandated.

    Records related to postmarket reports, including both periodic and adverse experience reports, must be retained for a period of at least 4 years from the date the report was submitted to FDA
    Found in 1114.45 (supplemental), which G does not cite.

  • SUPPORTED · type size and prominence · 740.2(a)
    in no case may the letters and/or numbers be less than 1/16 inch in height
    Found in 740.2 (supplemental), which G does not cite. 740.2(a) also requires prominence, conspicuousness and bold type on a contrasting background; 740.10(a) requires the statement on the principal display panel.

Research needed

  1. Retrieve the full 740.10(a) warning statement (dropped after the colon) and confirm it says the safety of the product has not been determined.
  2. If confirmed, correct G to '21 CFR 740.2; 740.10'.

source: batch reviewer, second pass (supplemental section text)

Supplemental 740.2 settles type size and prominence, which supports adding it to G. The core warning wording in 740.10(a) is still missing from the evidence, so the first element cannot be confirmed.materially uncertain (NEEDS_RESEARCH)

MEDIUM		Which ingredients are prohibited or restricted in cosmetics under Part 700?
key & evidence

G 21 CFR 700.11 through 700.35
H —
J Bithionol, mercury compounds, vinyl chloride propellants, halogenated salicylanilides, chloroform, methylene chloride, hexachlorophene, sunscreens in cosmetics.

  • SUPPORTED · Bithionol · 700.11(a)
    Accordingly, any cosmetic containing bithionol is deemed to be adulterated under section 601(a) of the Federal Food, Drug, and Cosmetic Act.
  • SUPPORTED · mercury compounds · 700.13(d)(2)
    will regard as adulterated within the meaning of section 601(a) of the Act any cosmetic containing mercury unless the cosmetic meets the conditions of paragraph (d)(2) (i) or (ii) of this section
    A restriction, not an outright ban: trace amounts under 1 ppm and eye-area preservative use up to 65 ppm are allowed ((d)(2)(i)-(ii)). Skin-bleaching use is addressed in (d)(1).
  • SUPPORTED · vinyl chloride propellants · 700.14(a)
    Accordingly, any cosmetic aerosol product containing vinyl chloride as an ingredient is deemed to be adulterated under section 601(a) of the Federal Food, Drug, and Cosmetic Act.
    Covers vinyl chloride as any ingredient of a cosmetic aerosol, including as propellant.
  • SUPPORTED · halogenated salicylanilides · 700.15(b)
    any cosmetic product that contains such a halogenated salicylanilide as an ingredient at any level for any purpose is deemed to be adulterated
    Limited to the four compounds named in 700.15(a) (TBS, DBS, MBS, TCSA).
  • SUPPORTED · chloroform · 700.18(b)
    Any cosmetic product containing chloroform as an ingredient is adulterated and is subject to regulatory action under sections 301 and 601(a)
    Residual processing-solvent amounts are excluded.
  • SUPPORTED · methylene chloride · 700.19(b)
    Any cosmetic product that contains methylene chloride as an ingredient is deemed adulterated
  • SUPPORTED · hexachlorophene · 250.250(e)
    Hexachlorophene may be used as a preservative in cosmetic products other than those which in normal use may be applied to mucous membranes
    The restriction exists (preservative only, max 0.1 percent, not for mucous-membrane products), but it is in 21 CFR 250.250(e), outside Part 700 and outside G. 700.11(a) mentions hexachlorophene only as a cross-sensitizer.
  • PARTIALLY_SUPPORTED · sunscreens in cosmetics · 700.35(a)
    the term must be qualified by describing the cosmetic benefit provided by the sunscreen ingredient.
    700.35 is a labeling-qualification rule for cosmetics that use the term sunscreen, not a prohibition or restriction of the ingredient.

Research needed

  1. Decide whether to add 21 CFR 250.250(e) to G, given that the question is scoped to Part 700 and the hexachlorophene restriction sits in Part 250.
  2. Decide whether 'sunscreens in cosmetics' belongs in a prohibited or restricted ingredient answer, since 700.35 only requires labeling qualification.
  3. Decide whether J's omission of zirconium aerosols (700.16), CFC propellants (700.23) and prohibited cattle materials (700.27) makes the key unusable as a complete answer.

Column J issue: J lists hexachlorophene as a Part 700 restriction, but its restriction is in 21 CFR 250.250(e), not Part 700. J lists sunscreens as a restricted ingredient, but 700.35 only requires the term 'sunscreen' to be qualified in cosmetic labeling. J omits zirconium aerosols (700.16), CFC propellants (700.23) and prohibited cattle materials (700.27), all of which are in the cited range.

source: batch reviewer, second pass (supplemental section text)

The supplemental text confirms the Part 700 restrictions for mercury, vinyl chloride, halogenated salicylanilides, chloroform and methylene chloride. Hexachlorophene is restricted only in 250.250(e), outside the Part 700 scope the question sets, and sunscreen is a labeling rule, so the row cannot be fixed by a citation change alone.materially uncertain (NEEDS_RESEARCH); column J not fully supported by text

    This shall be expressed in the terms of weight, measure, numerical count, or a combination or numerical count and weight, measure, or size.
    201.62(b), (i), (j), (m), (n) specify avoirdupois/U.S. gallon units and dual-unit formats.
  • SUPPORTED · Prominence of the net quantity statement · 201.62(g)
    The declaration shall appear in conspicuous and easily legible boldface print or type in distinct contrast
    201.62(h) sets minimum type sizes by principal display panel area.

source: batch reviewer, first pass

201.62 states placement (d)/(e), units (a)/(b)/(i)/(j) and prominence (g)/(h) of the net quantity declaration. Scope note: 201.62(a) addresses the label of an over-the-counter drug in package form, while the question says 'a drug label' generally.

key & evidence

G 21 CFR 50.50 through 50.56

J Risk and benefit categories, IRB findings, assent of the child and permission of parents or guardians.

  • SUPPORTED · Risk and benefit categories · 50.51-50.54
    in which no greater than minimal risk to children is presented may involve children as subjects only if the IRB finds that:
    Four categories: 50.51 (no greater than minimal risk), 50.52 (greater than minimal risk with prospect of direct benefit), 50.53 (minor increase over minimal risk, no direct benefit), 50.54 (not otherwise approvable, referred to the Commissioner).

  • SUPPORTED · Assent of the child · 50.55(a)
    the IRB must determine that adequate provisions are made for soliciting the assent of the children when in the judgment of the IRB the children are capable of providing assent.
    Exceptions and waiver in 50.55(c)-(d).
  • SUPPORTED · Permission of parents or guardians · 50.55(e)
    the IRB must determine, in accordance with and to the extent that consent is required under part 50, that the permission of each child
    One parent may suffice under 50.51 or 50.52; both parents are required under 50.53 or 50.54 (50.55(e)(1)-(2)).
